\Gamma_{N,j}(\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N})
,
and set

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}_{N}(\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N})=\big\{(\Gamma_{N,j}(\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}),\partial_{t}\Gamma_{N,j}(\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}))\big\}_{j=1}^{N}.

Then, putting (
3.5
)-(
3.12
) together,
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\vec{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}_{N}(\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N})\|_{X^{s}_{N}(\tau)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\big\|\big(v^{(0)}_{N,j},v^{(1)}_{N,j}\big)\big\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}+C_{2}\tau\|\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(\tau)}^{3}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{3}\tau\|\boldsymbol{Z}_{N}\|_{\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{\varepsilon}(T)}\Big(1+\|\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(\tau)}\Big)^{2}

for any
\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}\in X^{s}_{N}(\tau)
.
A similar computation yields the following difference estimate:

\displaystyle\|\vec{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}_{N}

\displaystyle(\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N})-\vec{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}_{N}(\vec{\mathbf{w}}_{N})\|_{X^{s}_{N}(\tau)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau\Big(1+\|\boldsymbol{Z}_{N}\|_{\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{\varepsilon}(T)}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(1+\|\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(\tau)}+\|\vec{\mathbf{w}}_{N}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(\tau)}\Big)^{2}\|\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}-\vec{\mathbf{w}}_{N}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(\tau)}

for any
\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N},\vec{\mathbf{w}}_{N}\in X^{s}_{N}(\tau)
.
Therefore,
by choosing

\tau=\tau\Big(\big\|\big(v^{(0)}_{N,j},v^{(1)}_{N,j}\big)\big\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}},\|\boldsymbol{Z}_{N}\|_{\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{\varepsilon}(T)})\Big)>0

sufficiently small,
we conclude that
\vec{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}_{N}
is a contraction in the (closed) ball

B_{R}\subset X_{N}^{s}(\tau)
of radius

R=2C_{1}\big\|\big(v^{(0)}_{N,j},v^{(1)}_{N,j}\big)\big\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}}+1
.
At this point, the uniqueness holds only in the ball
B_{R}

but by a standard continuity argument,
we can extend the uniqueness to hold
in the entire class
X_{N}^{s}(\tau)
.
We omit details.
This concludes the proof of Proposition
3.1
.
∎

3.2.
Preliminary estimates

In this subsection, we establish preliminary uniform (in
N
)
estimates for proving global well-posedness of
\text{HLSM}_{N}
(
1.10
)
by pathwise analysis.

Fix a target time
T\gg 1
.
In view of
the blowup alternative (
3.4
)
and
(
2.8
),
almost sure global well-posedness of (
3.1
) on
[0,T]

follows once we show
the following bound:

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|(Iv_{N,j},\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j})(t;\omega)\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{1}_{x}}\leq C(\omega,T)

(3.13)

for some almost surely finite constant
C(\omega,T)>0
,
where

\{Iv_{N,j}\}_{j=1}^{N}
is a solution to the following
I
-SdNLW system:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(\partial_{t}^{2}+\partial_{t}+m-\Delta)Iv_{N,j}\\
&\quad=-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(I(v_{N,k}^{2}v_{N,j})+2I(\Psi_{k}v_{N,k}v_{N,j})+I(v_{N,k}^{2}\Psi_{j})\\
&\hphantom{XXXXXX}+I(:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}}\!:v_{N,j})+2I(v_{N,k}:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:)+I(:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}\Psi_{j}}\!:)\Big).\end{split}

(3.14)

Here,
I
denotes the
I
-operator defined in (
2.7
).
Define
the modified energy
E_{N}(t)
by setting

\displaystyle E_{N}(t)=E_{N}(I\mathbf{v}_{N},\partial_{t}I\mathbf{v}_{N})(t),

(3.15)

where
E_{N}
is the energy defined in (
1.13
).
Then, (
3.13
) follows once we prove

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}E_{N}(t;\omega)\leq C^{\prime}(\omega,T)

for some almost surely finite constant
C^{\prime}(\omega,T)>0
.

Let
t_{2}\geq t_{1}\geq 0
.
From (
3.15
) and (
3.14
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}E_{N}(t_{2})-E_{N}(t_{1})&=\sum_{\ell=1}^{4}B_{\ell}-\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j})^{2}dxdt\\
&\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{4}B_{\ell},\end{split}

(3.16)

where
B_{\ell}=B_{\ell}(N)
,
\ell=1,\dots,4
,
are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}B_{1}&=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j}\Big((Iv_{N,k})^{2}Iv_{N,j}-I(v_{N,k}^{2}v_{N,j})\Big)dxdt\\
B_{2}&=-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j}\Big(2I(\Psi_{k}v_{N,k}v_{N,j})+I(v_{N,k}^{2}\Psi_{j})\Big)dxdt,\\
B_{3}&=-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j}\Big(I(:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}}\!:v_{N,j})+2I(v_{N,k}:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:)\Big)dxdt,\\
B_{4}&=-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j}\sum_{k=1}^{N}I(:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}\Psi_{j}}\!:)dxdt.\end{split}

(3.17)

In (
3.16
) and (
3.17
), we suppressed

t
-dependence for notational simplicity;
we apply the same convention in the following.
The first term
B_{1}
is the main commutator term, appearing
in the standard application of the
I
-method,
while the terms
B_{2}
,
B_{3}
, and
B_{4}

represent the contributions from the perturbative terms
in (
3.14
)
which are to be controlled
by a Gronwall-type argument.
As seen in
[
23
]
,
the key contribution from the perturbative terms
comes from
B_{2}

(or rather
B_{2}^{\prime}
defined in (
3.19
) below).

In the following,
we establish uniform (in
N\in\mathbb{N}
)
bounds on
B_{\ell}
,
\ell=1,\dots,4
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
\frac{2}{3}\leq s<1
. Then, we have

\displaystyle|B_{1}|\lesssim M^{2-3s}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}E_{N}^{2}(t)dt

(3.18)

for any
t_{2}\geq t_{1}\geq 0
,
where the implicit constant is independent of
N,M\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

The bound (
3.18
)
follows from
Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality in
x
,
Lemma
2.2
,
and
Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality in
j
and
k

with (
3.15
).
∎

By formally distributing the
I
-operator
to each factor of
B_{2}
and
B_{3}

in (
3.17
),
we obtain the following terms:

\displaystyle\begin{split}B_{2}^{\prime}&=-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j}\\
&\hphantom{XXXXX}\times\Big(2(I\Psi_{k})(Iv_{N,k})(Iv_{N,j})+(Iv_{N,k})^{2}I\Psi_{j}\Big)dxdt,\\
B_{3}^{\prime}&=-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j}\\
&\hphantom{XXXXX}\times\Big(\big(I(:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}}\!:)\big)Iv_{N,j}+2Iv_{N,k}\big(I(:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:)\big)\Big)dxdt.\end{split}

(3.19)

We first establish the following commutator estimates.

Lemma 3.4
.

For
\ell=2,3
,
let
B_{\ell}
and
B_{\ell}^{\prime}
be as in (
3.17
) and (
3.19
), respectively.
Let
\frac{2}{3}\leq s<1
.
Then, given
T\gg 1
and small
\delta>0
, we have

\displaystyle|B_{2}-B_{2}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\delta}V_{N}^{(1)}(T,\delta)\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}E_{N}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)dt,

(3.20)

\displaystyle|B_{3}-B_{3}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{-\frac{s}{2}+\delta}V_{N}^{(1)}(T,\delta)\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}E_{N}(t)dt

(3.21)

for any

0\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq T
and
any sufficiently large
M\gg 1
,
where the implicit constant is independent of

N\in\mathbb{N}
and
M,T\gg 1
.
Here,

V_{N}^{(1)}(T,\delta)

is defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}V_{N}^{(1)}(T,\delta)&=\|\Psi_{j}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\sigma_{0},p_{0}}}+\|:\!{\Psi_{j}^{2}}\!:\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\sigma_{0},p_{0}}}\\
&\quad+\|:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}^{(2)}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\sigma_{0},p_{0}}},\end{split}

(3.22)

where

\mathcal{A}_{N}B
and
\mathcal{A}_{N}^{(2)}B
are
the
\ell^{2}
-averages defined in (
1.11
) and (
2.2
),
while

\sigma_{0}=\sigma_{0}(\delta)>0
and
p_{0}=p_{0}(\delta)\gg 1
are as in Lemma
2.3
.

Proof.

The first bound (
3.20
) follows
from
Lemma
2.3

and Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality,
while the
second bound (
3.21
) follows
from
Lemma
2.3
,
Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality,
and (
2.1
).
∎

Next, we estimate

B_{4}
and
B_{3}^{\prime}
in (
3.17
) and (
3.19
), respectively.

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
B_{4}
and
B_{3}^{\prime}
be as in (
3.17
) and (
3.19
), respectively.
Let
\frac{2}{3}\leq s<1
.
Then, given small
\gamma>0
, we have

\displaystyle|B_{4}|

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{\gamma}V_{N}^{(2)}(T,\gamma)\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}E_{N}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)dt,

(3.23)

\displaystyle|B_{3}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{\gamma}V_{N}^{(2)}(T,\gamma)\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}E_{N}^{\frac{3}{4}}(t)dt

(3.24)

for any

0\leq t_{0}\leq t\leq T
and
any sufficiently large
M\gg 1
,
where the implicit constant is independent of

N\in\mathbb{N}
and
M,T\gg 1
.
Here,

V_{N}^{(2)}(T,\gamma)

is defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}V_{N}^{(2)}(T,\gamma)&=\|:\!{\Psi_{j}^{2}}\!:\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\gamma,4}}+\|:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}^{(2)}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\gamma,4}}\\
&\quad+\|:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}\Psi_{j}}\!:\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}^{(2)}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\gamma,4}},\end{split}

(3.25)

where

\mathcal{A}_{N}B
and
\mathcal{A}_{N}^{(2)}B
are
the
\ell^{2}
-averages defined
in (
1.11
) and (
2.2
).

Proof.

The first bound (
3.23
) on
B_{4}
follows
from
Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality
in
x
and then in
j

and (
2.1
).

As for the second bound (
3.24
) on
B_{3}^{\prime}
,
we first write

\displaystyle B_{3}^{\prime}=B_{31}^{\prime}+B_{32}^{\prime},

(3.26)

where

B_{31}^{\prime}
and
B_{32}^{\prime}
are given by

\displaystyle B_{31}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j}\cdot I(:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}}\!:)\cdot Iv_{N,j}dxdt,

\displaystyle B_{32}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j}\cdot Iv_{N,k}\cdot I(:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:)dxdt.

By Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality in
j
,
Hölder’s inequality in
x
,
(
3.15
),
(
2.9
),
and (
2.1
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}|B_{31}^{\prime}|&\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\|\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j}\|_{\ell_{j}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}\|Iv_{N,j}\|_{L_{x}^{4}\ell^{2}_{j}}\|I(:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}}\!:)\|_{L_{x}^{4}}dt^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim M^{\gamma}\|:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}}\!:\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\gamma,4}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}E_{N}^{\frac{3}{4}}(t)dt.\end{split}

(3.27)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}|B_{32}^{\prime}|&\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\|\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j}\|_{\ell_{j}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}\|Iv_{N,k}\|_{L_{x}^{4}\ell^{2}_{k}}\|I(:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:)\|_{\ell^{2}_{j,k}L_{x}^{4}}dt^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim M^{\gamma}\|:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}^{(2)}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\gamma,4}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}E_{N}^{\frac{3}{4}}(t)dt.\end{split}

(3.28)

Hence, the second bound (
3.24
)
follows from (
3.26
), (
3.27
), and (
3.28
)
with (
3.25
).
∎

Lastly, we
estimate
B_{2}^{\prime}

in (
3.19
),
where the logarithmically divergent nature
of
I\Psi_{j}
plays an important role.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
B_{2}^{\prime}
be as in (
3.19
).
Then, given
s<1
,
there exists
c>0
such that

\displaystyle|B_{2}^{\prime}|\lesssim\bigg\{\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\Big(1+E_{N}^{1+c\eta}(t)+\frac{\eta}{(t-t_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)dt\bigg\}\|I\Psi_{j}\|_{L^{\eta^{-1}}_{[t_{1},t_{2}],x}\mathcal{A}_{N}}

(3.29)

for any

t_{2}\geq t_{1}\geq 0

and small
\eta>0
,
uniformly in

M,N\in\mathbb{N}
,
where

L^{r}_{[t_{1},t_{2}],x}\mathcal{A}_{N}=L^{r}_{[t_{1},t_{2}]}L^{r}_{x}\mathcal{A}_{N}
.

Proof.

The bound (
3.29
) follows
from a straightforward modification
of the proof of

[
23
, Lemma 3.4]

adapted to the current vector-valued setting.
We first note that
by interpolation,
Minkowski’s integral inequality,
and Sobolev’s inequality with (
3.15
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|Iv_{N,j}\|_{L^{\frac{4}{1-2\eta}}_{x}\mathcal{A}_{N}}&\leq\|Iv_{N,j}\|_{L^{4}_{x}\mathcal{A}_{N}}^{1-4\eta}\|Iv_{N,j}\|_{L^{8}_{x}\mathcal{A}_{N}}^{4\eta}\leq\|Iv_{N,j}\|_{L^{4}_{x}\mathcal{A}_{N}}^{1-4\eta}\|Iv_{N,j}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}L^{8}_{x}}^{4\eta}\\
&\lesssim\|Iv_{N,j}\|_{L^{4}_{x}\mathcal{A}_{N}}^{1-4\eta}\|Iv_{N,j}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}H^{1}_{x}}^{4\eta}\\
&\lesssim E_{N}^{\frac{1-4\eta}{4}}E_{N}^{2\eta}=E_{N}^{\frac{1+4\eta}{4}},\end{split}

(3.30)

uniformly in small
\eta>0
.

Write
B_{2}^{\prime}
in (
3.19
)
as

\displaystyle B_{2}^{\prime}=B_{21}^{\prime}+B_{22}^{\prime},

where

B_{21}^{\prime}
and
B_{22}^{\prime}
are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}B_{21}^{\prime}&=-\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j}\cdot(I\Psi_{k})(Iv_{N,k})(Iv_{N,j})dxdt,\\
B_{22}^{\prime}&=-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j}\cdot(Iv_{N,k})^{2}\cdot I\Psi_{j}dxdt.\end{split}

(3.31)

By Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality (in
j
and
k
),
Hölder’s inequality (in
x
),
and (
3.30
), we have

\displaystyle|B_{22}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\|\partial_{t}Iv_{N,j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}\mathcal{A}_{N}}\|Iv_{N,k}\|_{L^{\frac{4}{1-2\eta}}_{x}\mathcal{A}_{N}}^{2}\|I\Psi_{j}\|_{L_{x}^{\eta^{-1}}\mathcal{A}_{N}}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}E_{N}^{1+2\eta}(t)\|I\Psi_{j}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\eta^{-1}}\mathcal{A}_{N}}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}E_{N}^{\frac{1+2\eta}{1-\eta}}(t)dt\bigg)^{1-\eta}\|I\Psi_{j}\|_{L^{\eta^{-1}}_{[t_{1},t_{2}],x}\mathcal{A}_{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg\{\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\Big(E_{N}^{\frac{1+2\eta}{1-2\eta}}(t)+\frac{\eta}{(t-t_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)dt\bigg\}\|I\Psi_{j}\|_{L^{\eta^{-1}}_{[t_{1},t_{2}],x}\mathcal{A}_{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg\{\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\Big(1+E_{N}^{1+c\eta}(t)+\frac{\eta}{(t-t_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)dt\bigg\}\|I\Psi_{j}\|_{L^{\eta^{-1}}_{[t_{1},t_{2}],x}\mathcal{A}_{N}},

uniformly in small
\eta>0
,
where the penultimate step follows from
[
23
, the math display before (3.21)]

as a consequence of Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities.
The contribution from
B_{21}^{\prime}
in (
3.31
)
can be treated in an analogous manner,
thus yielding (
3.29
).
∎

3.3.
Global well-posedness

In this subsection, we prove
pathwise global well-posedness of
\text{HLSM}_{N}
(
1.10
)
(Theorem
1.1
(ii)).
In view of the first order expansion (
1.7
),
Theorem
1.1
(ii)
follows once we prove the following proposition.

Proposition 3.7
.

Let
m>0
and
\frac{4}{5}<s<1
.
Given
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
\big(\mathbf{v}_{N}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}_{N}^{(1)}\big)=\big\{\big(v_{N,j}^{(0)},v_{N,j}^{(1)}\big)\big\}_{j=1}^{N}\in\big(\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\big)^{\otimes N}
. Then, there exists a unique solution

(\mathbf{v}_{N},\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{N})=\{(v_{N,j},\partial_{t}v_{N,j})\}_{j=1}^{N}

in the class

\big(C(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))\big)^{\otimes N}

to (
3.1
) with initial data

\big(\mathbf{v}_{N}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}_{N}^{(1)}\big)
,
almost surely.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to a proof of Proposition
3.7
,
with a particular emphasis on uniformity in
N\in\mathbb{N}
;
see, for example, Remark
3.9
.
Fix
\frac{4}{5}<s<1
.
Given small
\delta,\gamma>0
and a target time
T\gg 1
,
it follows from
(
3.16
), (
3.17
), (
3.19
), Lemmas
3.3
,
3.4
,
3.5
, and
3.6

that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{N}(t_{2})-E_{N}(t_{1})\\
&\quad\lesssim M^{2-3s}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}E_{N}^{2}(t)dt\\
&\quad\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{2}M^{-\frac{1-\ell(1-s)}{2}+\delta}V_{N}^{(1)}(T,\delta)\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}E_{N}^{\frac{\ell+1}{2}}(t)dt\\
&\quad\quad+M^{\gamma}V_{N}^{(2)}(T,\gamma)\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\big(1+E_{N}^{\frac{3}{4}}(t)\big)dt\\
&\quad\quad+\bigg\{\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\Big(1+E_{N}^{1+c\eta}(t)+\frac{\eta}{(t-t_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)dt\bigg\}\|I\Psi_{j}\|_{L^{\eta^{-1}}_{[t_{1},t_{2}],x}\mathcal{A}_{N}}\end{split}

(3.32)

for any
0\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq T

and small
\eta>0
,
uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
M\gg 1
,
where
V_{N}^{(1)}(T,\delta)
and

V_{N}^{(2)}(T,\gamma)

are as in (
3.22
) and (
3.25
), respectively.

Before we state a crucial lemma (Lemma
3.8
)
that allows us to proceed with an iterative argument,
we introduce two auxiliary random variables.
Given
N\in\mathbb{N}
, let
R_{N}
be as in Lemma
2.7

which controls
I\Psi_{j}
on the last term in (
3.32
).
The next random
V_{N}
controls
the other
contributions from the stochastic terms in (
3.32
).
By choosing
\varepsilon=\min(\sigma_{0},\gamma)
,
it follows from (
3.22
) and (
3.25
)
that

\displaystyle V_{N}^{(1)}(T,\delta)+V_{N}^{(2)}(T,\gamma)\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{N}\|_{\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{\varepsilon}(T)},

where

\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{\varepsilon}(T)
is as
in (
2.17
)
and
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{N}
is given by

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{N}=\big\{\Psi_{j},\,:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:\,,\,:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}\Psi_{j}}\!:\big\}_{j,k=1}^{N}.

Define a random variable
V_{N}
by

\displaystyle e^{V_{N}^{\frac{1}{3}}}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}e^{-\theta\ell}e^{\|\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{N}\|_{\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}(\ell)}^{\frac{1}{3}}}

(3.33)

for some
\theta>0
.
Then, by applying
Lemma
2.5
(ii)
and choosing
\theta>0
sufficiently large, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{V_{N}^{\frac{1}{3}}}\Big]=\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}e^{-\theta\ell}\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{\|\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{N}\|_{\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}(\ell)}^{\frac{1}{3}}}\Big]\lesssim\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}e^{-\theta\ell}e^{c\ell}<\infty.

(3.34)

From (
3.33
), we also have

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{N}\|_{\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{\varepsilon}(T)}\leq\Big(V_{N}^{\frac{1}{3}}+\theta(1+T)\Big)^{3}\lesssim V_{N}+T^{3}.

(3.35)

The left-hand side of (
3.35
)
plays a role of
M_{T}

in
[
23
, (3.24)]
.

The following lemma
is an analogue of

[
23
, Proposition 3.5]

in the current vector-valued setting.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
\frac{2}{3}<s<1

and
T\gg 1
.
Given
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
R_{N}
and
V_{N}
be as in Lemma
2.7

and (
3.33
), respectively,
which are almost surely finite.
Fix
\omega\in\Omega

such that
R_{N}(\omega),V_{N}(\omega)<\infty
.
Then, there exist
\alpha=\alpha(s)
,
\beta=\beta(s)>0
,
and
M_{*}=M_{*}(s)\in\mathbb{N}
,
satisfying

\displaystyle\beta<\alpha\leq 3s-2,

(3.36)

such that
if

\displaystyle E_{N}(t_{0})\leq M^{\beta}

(3.37)

for some
0\leq t_{0}<T

and
M\geq M_{*}
,
then
there exists
small

\tau_{N}=\tau_{N}(s,M,T,R_{N}(\omega),V_{N}(\omega))=\tau_{N}(s,M,T,\omega)>0

such that

\displaystyle E_{N}(t)\leq M^{\alpha}

for any
t

satisfying

t_{0}\leq t\leq\min(T,t_{0}+\tau_{N})
.

Remark 3.9
.

In Lemma
3.8
,
the dependence of
\tau_{N}
on
N

only appears through

R_{N}
and
V_{N}
.
In view of
the uniform (in
N
) bounds
(
2.26
)
and
(
3.34
)
with Chebyshev’s inequality,
we see that
Lemma
3.8

holds “uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
”
in the sense that the statistical properties
of
\tau_{N}
can be
estimated
uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof of Lemma
3.8
.

Once we have the bound (
3.32
),
this proposition follows from repeating
the proof of

[
23
, Proposition 3.5]

based on a
double Gronwall-type inequality
and we only indicate the basic idea.
As in
[
23
, (3.32) and (3.38)]
,
define
F_{N}
by

\displaystyle\begin{split}F_{N}(t)&=\max_{t_{0}\leq\tau\leq t}E_{N}(\tau)-E_{N}(t_{0})+\max(E_{N}(t_{0}),M^{\beta}),\\
G_{N}(t)&=F_{N}(t)-2C_{0}R_{N}(t-t_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\end{split}

(3.38)

for some suitable
C_{0}>0
.
Then, after some manipulation on
(
3.32
) with (
3.38
),
we arrive at

\displaystyle\begin{split}G_{N}(t)-G_{N}(t_{0})\lesssim(1+V_{N}+R_{N}+T)\int_{t_{0}}^{t}G_{N}(t^{\prime})(\log G(t^{\prime})+T^{2})dt^{\prime}\end{split}

(3.39)

for any
t_{0}\leq t\leq\min(t_{1},t_{0}+t_{*}(C_{0},R_{N}))

such that

2C_{0}R(t-t_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\sim 1
,
guaranteeing

G_{N}(t)\sim F_{N}(t)
;
see
[
23
, (3.39)]
.
From the differential inequality (
3.39
),
we obtain a double exponential bound on
G_{N}(t)
,
which, together with (
3.37
),
implies the conclusion of this proposition.
Lastly, we note that
the condition (
3.36
) follows from

[
23
, (3.36)]

and that
the condition

M\geq M_{*}=M_{*}(s)

comes from

[
23
, (3.45) and (3.46)]
.
∎

We conclude this section by presenting
a proof of
Proposition
3.7
.

Proof of Proposition
3.7
.

This proposition follows from
a straightforward modification of the proof of

[
23
, Theorem 1.2]
.
We, however, present some details
in order to demonstrate that the following iterative argument
provides
a uniform (in
N
) bound on the
\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}
-norm
of a solution
\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}(t)=(\mathbf{v}_{N},\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{N})(t)
.
See also Remark
3.10
.

Let
\frac{4}{5}<s<1

and fix a target time
T\gg 1
.
Moreover,
given
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
fix

\omega\in\Omega

such that

\displaystyle R_{N}(\omega),V_{N}(\omega)<\infty.

(3.40)

Then, let the parameters
\alpha,\beta,M_{*}
and
\tau_{N}

be as in Lemma
3.8
.

Fix
M_{0}\gg 1
(to be determined later)
and define an increasing sequence
\{M_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
by setting

\displaystyle M_{k}=M_{0}^{\sigma^{k}}

for some
\sigma\gg 1
.
Under the assumption

\displaystyle 2(1-s)<\beta,

(3.41)

we can take

\sigma=\sigma(s,\alpha,\beta)\gg 1

sufficiently large such that

\displaystyle M_{k+1}^{2(1-s)}M_{k}^{\alpha}+M_{k}^{2\alpha}\ll M_{k+1}^{\beta},

(3.42)

We point out that the constraint
s>\frac{4}{5}

appears
from (
3.36
)
and (
3.41
).

Given
N,M\in\mathbb{N}

and
t\geq 0
, set

\displaystyle E_{N,M}(t)=E_{N}(I_{M}\mathbf{v}_{N},\partial_{t}I_{M}\mathbf{v}_{N})(t)

(3.43)

where the right-hand side is as in (
1.13
).
Suppose that

\displaystyle E_{N,M_{k}}(t)\leq M_{k}^{\alpha}

(3.44)

for some
k\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and
t\geq 0
. Then, by (
2.8
), Minkowski’s integral inequality,
(
2.9
), Sobolev’s inequality (with
s\geq\frac{1}{2}
), (
3.44
), and (
3.42
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}E_{N,M_{k+1}}(t)&\lesssim\|(I_{M_{k+1}}\mathbf{v}_{N},\partial_{t}I_{M_{k+1}}\mathbf{v}_{N})\|^{2}_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|I_{M_{k+1}}\mathbf{v}_{N}\|^{4}_{L_{x}^{4}\mathcal{A}_{N}}\\
&\lesssim M_{k+1}^{2(1-s)}\|(\mathbf{v}_{N},\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{N})\|^{2}_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}_{x}^{s}}+\|I_{M_{k+1}}\mathbf{v}_{N}\|^{4}_{\mathcal{A}_{N}L_{x}^{4}}\\
&\lesssim M_{k+1}^{2(1-s)}\|(I_{M_{k}}\mathbf{v}_{N},\partial_{t}I_{M_{k}}\mathbf{v}_{N})\|^{2}_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{N}\|^{4}_{\mathcal{A}_{N}H_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\\
&\lesssim M_{k+1}^{2(1-s)}M_{k}^{\alpha}+\|I_{M_{k}}\mathbf{v}_{N}\|^{4}_{\mathcal{A}_{N}H_{x}^{1}}\\
&\lesssim M_{k+1}^{2(1-s)}M_{k}^{\alpha}+M_{k}^{2\alpha}\\
&\ll M_{k+1}^{\beta},\end{split}

(3.45)

where, at the fourth inequality, we use the fact that
s\geq\frac{1}{2}

(in applying (
2.8
)).

Given
\big(\mathbf{v}_{N}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}_{N}^{(1)}\big)\in\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}_{x}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})

and
\omega\in\Omega
, satisfying (
3.40
),
let

\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}(\omega)=(\mathbf{v}_{N}(\omega),\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{N}(\omega))

be the local-in-time solution to (
3.1
)
with
\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}(\omega)|_{t=0}=\big(\mathbf{v}_{N}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}_{N}^{(1)}\big)
.
In view of
the blowup alternative (
3.4
),
the desired global well-posedness of (
3.1
)
on the time interval
[0,T]

(for the fixed
\omega\in\Omega
) follows
once we prove

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}(t;\omega)\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}\leq C(\omega,T)<\infty.

(3.46)

In the following, we prove (
3.46
)
by implementing an iterative argument
based on Lemma
3.8
.

Choose

M_{0}=M_{0}\big(\mathbf{v}_{N}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}_{N}^{(1)},s,T\big)\gg 1

such that

\displaystyle E_{N,M_{0}}(0)\leq M_{0}^{\beta}.

Then, it follows from Lemma
3.8

that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq\tau_{N}}E_{N,M_{0}}(t)\leq M_{0}^{\alpha},

satisfying (
3.44
) at time
\tau_{N}
.
From
(
3.43
),
(
1.13
),
and (
2.8
),
this in particular implies

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq\tau_{N}}\|\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}(t;\omega)\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}^{2}\lesssim M_{0}^{\alpha}.

Moreover,
from (
3.45
),
we obtain

\displaystyle E_{N,M_{1}}(\tau_{N})\leq M_{1}^{\beta}.

By applying
Lemma
3.8
once again,
we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau_{N}\leq t\leq 2\tau_{N}}E_{N,M_{1}}(t)\leq M_{1}^{\alpha},

satisfying (
3.44
) at time
2\tau_{N}
,
from which we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau_{N}\leq t\leq 2\tau_{N}}\|\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}(t;\omega)\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}^{2}\lesssim M_{1}^{\alpha}

and

\displaystyle E_{N,M_{2}}(2\tau_{N})\leq M_{2}^{\beta}.

By iterating this argument
\big[\frac{T}{\tau_{N}}\big]+1
times, we
obtain the following bound:

\displaystyle\sup_{\ell\tau_{N}\leq t\leq(\ell+1)\tau_{N}}\|\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}(t;\omega)\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}^{2}\lesssim M_{\ell}^{\alpha}

for
\ell=0,\dots,\big[\frac{T}{\tau_{N}}\big]+1
,
yielding (
3.46
).
This proves existence of the unique solution
\mathbf{v}_{N}
to (
3.1
)
on the time interval
[0,T]

(for the fixed
\omega\in\Omega
, satisfying (
3.40
)).
Since the choice of
T\gg 1

was arbitrary,
we conclude almost sure global well-posedness of (
3.1
).
∎

Remark 3.10
.

As pointed out in

[
23
, Remark 3.7]
,
the iterative argument presented above
yields
the following uniform (in
N
)
double exponential bound:

\displaystyle\|\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}\leq C\exp\Big(c\log\big(2+\|\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{N}(0)\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}\big)\cdot e^{C(\omega)t^{2}}\Big)

for any
t\geq 0
.
See
[
23
, Remark 3.7]
for details.

4.
Well-posedness of the mean-field SdNLW

In this section, we present a proof of Theorem
1.2

on local and global well-posedness of the mean-field SdNLW (
1.20
). By the first order expansion (
1.19
),
we study the following equation
(see (
1.18
))
satisfied by the residual part
v=u-\Phi
, where we drop the superscript
j

for notational simplicity:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(\partial_{t}^{2}+\partial_{t}+m-\Delta)v=-\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]v-2\mathbb{E}[\Psi v]v-\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]\Psi-2\mathbb{E}[v\Psi]\Psi\\
(v,\partial_{t}v)|_{t=0}=(v^{(0)},v^{(1)}).\end{cases}

(4.1)

As pointed out in Section
1
,
there are no terms involving
\mathbb{E}[\,:\!{\Psi^{2}}\!:\,]

since
\mathbb{E}[\,:\!{\Psi^{2}}\!:\,]=0
.
We note that there are certain similarities between (
4.1
) and (
3.1
)
after replacing the empirical average

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{1\leq k\leq N}
in (
3.1
)
with the expectation
\mathbb{E}
,
leading to computations similar to those
presented in Section
3
.
There are, however,
fundamental differences
between
the mean-field SdNLW (
4.1
) and the SdNLW system
(
3.1
), in particular on the treatment of the following terms:

\mathbb{E}[v\Psi]\Psi\qquad\text{and}\qquad\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}v_{N,k}:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:\,,

where the former term does
not
have an explicit renormalization;
see (
4.6
) below.
See also the proofs of Lemmas
4.4
,
4.5
,
4.6
, and
4.7
,
where we apply a similar idea.

In Subsection
4.1
, we prove local well-posedness of (
4.1
).
In Subsection
4.2
, we briefly discuss
global well-posedness of (
4.1
).

4.1.
Local well-posedness

In this subsection,
we prove the following proposition on local well-posedness
of the mean-field SdNLW (
4.1
)
(Theorem
1.2
(i))
whose proof follows closely that of Proposition
3.1

but with one twist due to the term
\mathbb{E}[v\Psi]\Psi
;
see (
4.4
) below.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
m>0
and
\frac{1}{2}\leq s<1
. Given
2\leq p<\infty
,
let
(v^{(0)},v^{(1)})\in L^{p}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
.
Then, given any
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
there exists a unique solution
(v,\partial_{t}v)\in L^{p}(\Omega;C([t_{0},t_{0}+\tau];\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})))

to (
4.1
)
with
(v,\partial_{t}v)|_{t=t_{0}}=(v^{(0)},v^{(1)})
,
where the local existence time
\tau>0
depends
on

\|(v^{(0)},v^{(1)})\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}
and
\Psi
.

Remark 4.2
.

(i)
As pointed out in Remark
3.2
(i),
it is possible to lower the regularity threshold
by using the Strichartz estimates.
We, however,
restrict our attention to the range
\frac{1}{2}\leq s<1
,
which guarantees
unconditional uniqueness
of solutions
(namely, uniqueness
in the entire class

L^{p}(\Omega;C(\tau;\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})))
),
since it
plays a key role in Section
6
.

(ii) Let
T\geq 1
.
Let
(v,\partial_{t}v)
be the local-in-time solution
to (
4.1
) constructed in Proposition
4.1
.
Then,
by denoting by
T_{*}
its maximal existence time,
the following blowup alternative holds:

\displaystyle T_{*}=T\qquad\text{or}\qquad\lim_{t\to T_{*}-}\|(v,\partial_{t}v)(t)\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}=\infty.

(4.2)

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
t_{0}=0
.
By writing (
4.1
) in the Duhamel formulation,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}v(t)&=S(t)(v^{(0)},v^{(1)})-\mathcal{I}\big(\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]v\big)(t)-2\mathcal{I}\big(\mathbb{E}[\Psi v]v\big)(t)\\
&\quad-\mathcal{I}\big(\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]\Psi\big)(t)-2\mathcal{I}\big(\mathbb{E}[v\Psi]\Psi\big)(t),\\
\end{split}

(4.3)

where
S(t)
and
\mathcal{I}

are as in (
2.5
) and
(
2.6
), respectively.

Let
2\leq p<\infty
.
Fix
0<\tau\leq 1

and small
\varepsilon>0

such that
s\leq 1-\varepsilon
.
Let us first consider the last term on the right-hand side
of (
4.3
),
involving
\mathbb{E}[v\Psi]\Psi
,
which shows a new aspect as compared to
the hyperbolic
O(N)
linear sigma model
(
3.1
) studied in Subsection
3.1
.
In order to handle this term,
we
write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[v\Psi]\Psi(\omega)=\mathbb{E}[v^{\prime}\Psi^{\prime}]\Psi(\omega)=\int_{\Omega}v^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime})\Psi^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime})\Psi(\omega)\mathbb{P}(d\omega^{\prime}),

(4.4)

where
(v^{\prime}\Psi^{\prime})
is an independent copy of
(v,\Psi)
.
In particular, the product

\Psi^{\prime}\Psi
makes sense
without
renormalization.
As a consequence, it follows from Lemma
2.5

that

\displaystyle K_{p}=\|\Psi\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}C_{1}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}+\|\Psi^{\prime}\Psi\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}C_{1}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}<\infty.

(4.5)

Recall the notation (
3.7
).
We apply (
4.4
)
and proceed as in (
3.10
)
with
Sobolev’s inequality
and
Lemma
2.1
(ii).
Then, from
the independence of
\Psi^{\prime}
and
\Psi

with
\Law(v^{\prime})=\Law(v)

and (
4.5
),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|\vec{\mathcal{I}}\big(\mathbb{E}[v\Psi]\Psi\big)\big\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}_{x}^{s}}\lesssim\tau\big\|\|v^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime})\Psi^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime})\Psi(\omega)\|_{C_{\tau}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\frac{2}{2-s-\varepsilon}}}\big\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}L^{1}_{\omega^{\prime}}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\tau\Big\|\|\Psi^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime})\Psi(\omega)\|_{C_{1}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}\|v^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime})\|_{C_{\tau}W_{x}^{\varepsilon,\frac{2}{2-s-\varepsilon}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}L^{1}_{\omega^{\prime}}}\\
&\quad\leq\tau\|\Psi^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime})\Psi(\omega)\|_{L^{p}_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}C_{1}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}\|v^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega^{\prime}}C_{\tau}W_{x}^{\varepsilon,\frac{2}{2-s-\varepsilon}}}\\
&\quad=\tau\|\Psi^{\prime}(\omega)\Psi(\omega)\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}C_{1}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}C_{\tau}W_{x}^{\varepsilon,\frac{2}{2-s-\varepsilon}}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\tau K_{p}\|v\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}C_{\tau}H_{x}^{s}},\end{split}

(4.6)

provided that
\varepsilon\leq s\leq 1-\varepsilon
,
where, at the third inequality, we used the fact that
p\geq 2
.

Let us now handle the remaining terms.
Proceeding as in (
3.6
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\vec{\mathcal{I}}\big(\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]v\big)\big\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}_{x}^{s}}&\lesssim\tau\|\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]v\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{\tau}H_{x}^{s-1}}\\
&\lesssim\tau\Big\|\mathbb{E}\big[\|v\|^{2}_{C_{\tau}L_{x}^{\frac{6}{2-s}}}\big]\|v\|_{C_{\tau}L_{x}^{\frac{6}{2-s}}}\Big\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}}\\
&\lesssim\tau\|v\|^{3}_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{\tau}H_{x}^{s}},\end{split}

(4.7)

provided that
\frac{1}{2}\leq s
(
<1
).
Proceeding as in (
3.8
) with Lemma
2.1
(ii)
(but in a slightly different order due to the presence of the expectation),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\vec{\mathcal{I}}\big(\mathbb{E}[\Psi v]v\big)\big\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}_{x}^{s}}&\lesssim\tau\|\mathbb{E}[\Psi v]v\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{\tau}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\frac{2}{2-s-\varepsilon}}}\\
&\lesssim\tau\Big\|\mathbb{E}\big[\|\Psi v\|_{C_{\tau}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\frac{4}{2-s-\varepsilon}}}\big]\|v\|_{C_{1}W_{x}^{\varepsilon,\frac{4}{2-s-\varepsilon}}}\Big\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}}\\
&\lesssim\tau\|\Psi\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{1}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}\|v\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{\tau}H_{x}^{\frac{s}{2}+\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon}}^{2}\\
&\lesssim\tau K_{p}\|v\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{\tau}H_{x}^{s}}^{2},\end{split}

(4.8)

provided that
3\varepsilon\leq s
(
<1
).
Similarly,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\vec{\mathcal{I}}\big(\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]\Psi\big)\big\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}_{x}^{s}}&\lesssim\tau\|\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]\Psi\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{\tau}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\frac{2}{2-s-\varepsilon}}}\\
&\lesssim\tau\Big\|\mathbb{E}\big[\|v^{2}\|_{C_{\tau}W_{x}^{\varepsilon,\frac{2}{2-s-\varepsilon}}}\big]\|\Psi\|_{C_{1}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}\Big\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}}\\
&\lesssim\tau\|\Psi\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{1}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}\|v\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{\tau}H_{x}^{\frac{s}{2}+\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon}}^{2}\\
&\lesssim\tau K_{p}\|v\|_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{\tau}H_{x}^{s}}^{2},\end{split}

(4.9)

provided that
3\varepsilon\leq s
(
<1
).

Denote the right-hand side of (
4.3
)
by
\Gamma(\vec{v})=\Gamma(v,\partial_{t}v)
,
and set

\vec{\Gamma}(\vec{v})=\big(\Gamma(\vec{v}),\partial_{t}\Gamma(\vec{v})\big)
.
Then, putting (
4.3
), (
4.6
), (
4.7
),
(
4.8
), and (
4.9
) together,
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\vec{\Gamma}(\vec{v})\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\|(v^{(0)},v^{(1)})\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}+C_{2}\tau\|\vec{v}\|^{3}_{L_{\omega}^{p}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}_{x}^{s}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{3}\tau K_{p}\Big(1+\|\vec{v}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}_{x}^{s}}\Big)\|\vec{v}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}_{x}^{s}}

for any
\vec{v}\in L^{p}(\Omega;C([0,\tau];\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})))
,
where
K_{p}

is as in (
4.5
).
A similar computation yields the following difference estimate:

\displaystyle\|\vec{\Gamma}(\vec{v})-\vec{\Gamma}(\vec{w})\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\tau\Big(\|\vec{v}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}^{2}+\|\vec{w}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}^{2}\Big)\|\vec{v}-\vec{w}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\tau K_{p}\Big(1+\|\vec{v}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}+\|\vec{w}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}\Big)\|\vec{v}-\vec{w}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}C_{\tau}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}

for any
\vec{v},\vec{w}\in L^{p}(\Omega;C([0,\tau];\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})))
.
Therefore,
by choosing

\tau=\tau\big(\|(v^{(0)},v^{(1)})\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}},K_{p}\big)>0

sufficiently small,
we conclude that
\vec{\Gamma}
is a contraction in the
(closed) ball

B_{R}\subset L^{p}(\Omega;C([0,\tau];\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})))

of radius

R=2C_{1}\|(v^{(0)},v^{(1)})\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}+1
.
At this point, the uniqueness holds only in the ball
B_{R}

but by a standard continuity argument,
we can extend the uniqueness to hold
in the entire class
L^{p}(\Omega;C([0,\tau];\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})))
.
We omit details.
This concludes the proof of Proposition
4.1
.
∎

4.2.
Global well-posedness

In this subsection,
we prove global well-posedness of the mean-field SdNLW
(
1.20
) (Theorem
1.2
(ii)).
As in Subsection
3.3
,
our main strategy is to adapt the
hybrid
I
-method
introduced in
[
23
]

to the current mean-field setting.
We note that, while the analysis in
[
23
]

and Section
3

is pathwise,
our analysis presented below
is carried out under the
L^{2}(\Omega)
-norm (i.e. not pathwise).

Fix a target time
T\gg 1
.
In view of
the blowup alternative (
4.2
) (with
p=2
)
and
(
2.8
),
existence of the unique solution
(v,\partial_{t}v)
to (
4.1
) on
the time interval
[0,T]

follows once we show the following bound:

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|(Iv,\partial_{t}Iv)(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}\mathcal{H}^{1}_{x}}\leq C(T)

for some finite constant
C(T)>0
,
where
(Iv,\partial_{t}Iv)
is a solution to the following

I
-mean-field SdNLW:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(\partial_{t}^{2}+\partial_{t}+m-\Delta)Iv\\
&\quad=-I\big(\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]v\big)-2I\big(\mathbb{E}[\Psi v]v\big)-I\big(\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]\Psi\big)-2I\big(\mathbb{E}[v\Psi]\Psi\big).\end{split}

(4.10)

For this purpose, define
the modified energy

\mathcal{E}(Iv,\partial_{t}Iv)
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{E}(Iv,\partial_{t}Iv)&=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}|\nabla Iv|^{2}+m(Iv)^{2}+(\partial_{t}Iv)^{2}dx\bigg]+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big(\mathbb{E}[(Iv)^{2}]\big)^{2}dx\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\|\nabla Iv\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}}^{2}+m\|Iv\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}Iv\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}}^{2}dx+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\|Iv\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}}^{4}dx.\end{split}

(4.11)

where

\mathcal{E}(v,\partial_{t}v)
is as in (
1.22
).
For notational simplicity, we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)=\mathcal{E}(Iv,\partial_{t}Iv)(t).

As in Subsection
3.3
,
Theorem
1.2
(ii) on global well-posedness
of the mean-field SdNLW (
1.20
) follows
from the following proposition;
compare (
4.12
) with (
3.32
).

Proposition 4.3
.

(i)

Let
\frac{2}{3}\leq s<1
.
Then, given small
\delta,\gamma>0
and
T\gg 1
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{E}(t_{2})-\mathcal{E}(t_{1})&\lesssim M^{2-3s}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathcal{E}^{2}(t)dt\\
&\quad+M^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\delta}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathcal{E}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)dt\cdot\|\Psi\|_{L_{\omega}^{2}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\sigma_{0},p_{0}}}\\
&\quad+M^{-\frac{s}{2}+\delta}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathcal{E}(t)dt\cdot\|\Psi^{\prime}\Psi\|_{L_{\omega}^{2}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\sigma_{0},p_{0}}}\\
&\quad+M^{\gamma}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathcal{E}^{\frac{3}{4}}(t)dt\cdot\|\Psi^{\prime}\Psi\|_{L_{\omega}^{2}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\gamma,\infty}}\\
&\quad+\bigg\{\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\Big(1+\mathcal{E}^{1+c\eta}(t)+\frac{\eta}{(t-t_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)dt\bigg\}\|I\Psi\|_{L^{\eta^{-1}}_{[t_{1},t_{2}],x}L^{2}_{\omega}}\end{split}

(4.12)

for any
0\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq T

and small
\eta>0
,
uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
M,T\gg 1
,
where

\sigma_{0}=\sigma_{0}(\delta)>0
and
p_{0}=p_{0}(\delta)\gg 1
are as in Lemma
2.3
.
Here,
\Psi^{\prime}
denotes an independent copy of
\Psi
.

(ii)

Let
\frac{4}{5}<s<1
.
Then, given
(v^{(0)},v^{(1)})\in\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
,
there exists a unique global solution
(v,\partial_{t}v)\in C(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))

to
(
4.1
) with
(v,\partial_{t}v)|_{t=0}=(v^{(0)},v^{(1)})
.

Proof.

(i)
Let
t_{2}\geq t_{1}\geq 0
.
Then, from (
4.11
) and (
4.10
),
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t_{2})-\mathcal{E}(t_{1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\mathcal{B}_{\ell}-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{t}Iv)^{2}dxdt\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\mathcal{B}_{\ell},

where
\mathcal{B}_{\ell}=\mathcal{B}_{\ell}(N)
,
\ell=1,\dots,3
,
are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{1}&=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{t}Iv)\Big(\mathbb{E}[(Iv)^{2}]Iv-I\big(\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]v\big)\Big)dxdt\bigg],\\
\mathcal{B}_{2}&=-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{t}Iv)\Big(2I\big(\mathbb{E}[\Psi v]v\big)+I\big(\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]\Psi\big)\Big)dxdt\bigg],\\
\mathcal{B}_{3}&=-2\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{t}Iv)I\big(\mathbb{E}[v\Psi]\Psi\big)dxdt^{\prime}\bigg].\end{split}

(4.13)

Then,
the energy growth bound (
4.12
)
follows from
Lemmas
4.4
,
4.5
,

4.6
, and
4.7

presented below.

(ii)
The energy growth bound (
4.12
)
yields
an analogue of
Lemma
3.8
,
which in turn allows us to proceed
with an iterative argument as in the
proof of Proposition
3.7

presented in Subsection
3.3
.
We omit details.
∎

The remaining part of this section
is devoted
to estimating

\mathcal{B}_{\ell}
,
\ell=1,\dots,3
,
which
closely
follows the argument presented in
Subsection
3.2

but
with
modification coming from the viewpoint (
4.4
);
more precisely, as in (
4.4
),
we regard the outer expectation
as an integration
over
\omega\in\Omega
,
while
the inner expectation
is over
\omega^{\prime}\in\Omega
.
Moreover,
as in
Subsection
3.2
,
we often suppress

t
-dependence for notational simplicity
in the following.

The following lemma establishes
the commutator estimate
on the cubic term in
v
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\frac{2}{3}\leq s<1
. Then, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{1}|\lesssim M^{2-3s}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathcal{E}^{2}(t)dt

(4.14)

for any
t_{2}\geq t_{1}\geq 0
,
where the implicit constant is independent of
M\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

By Cauchy-Schwarz’s and Minkowski’s integral inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|(\partial_{t}Iv)\Big(\mathbb{E}[(Iv)^{2}]Iv-I\big(\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]v\big)\Big)\Big\|_{L^{1}_{\omega,x}}\\
&\quad\leq\|\partial_{t}Iv\|_{L^{2}_{\omega,x}}\big\|\mathbb{E}[(Iv)^{2}]Iv-I\big(\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]v\big)\big\|_{L^{2}_{\omega,x}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|\|(Iv(\omega^{\prime}))^{2}Iv(\omega)-I\big(v^{2}(\omega^{\prime})v(\omega)\big)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\Big\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}L^{1}_{\omega^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

(4.15)

Then, the bound (
4.14
)
follows
from (
4.15
),
Lemma
2.2
, and (
4.11
).
∎

By formally distributing the
I
-operator to each factor of
\mathcal{B}_{2}

and
\mathcal{B}_{3}

in (
4.13
),
we obtain the following term:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\prime}&=-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{t}Iv)\Big(2\mathbb{E}[(I\Psi)(Iv)]Iv+\mathbb{E}[(Iv)^{2}]I\Psi\Big)dxdt\bigg],\\
\mathcal{B}_{3}^{\prime}&=-2\mathbb{E}_{\omega}\bigg[\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{t}Iv(\omega))\,\mathbb{E}_{\omega^{\prime}}\Big[(Iv^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime}))\big(I(\Psi^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime})\Psi(\omega))\big)\Big]dxdt\bigg],\end{split}

(4.16)

where
(\Psi^{\prime},v^{\prime})
is an independent copy of
(\Psi,v)

as in (
4.4
).
We first establish the following commutator estimates.

Lemma 4.5
.

For
\ell=2,3
,
let
\mathcal{B}_{\ell}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\ell}^{\prime}
be as in (
4.13
) and (
4.16
), respectively.
Let
\frac{2}{3}\leq s<1
.
Then, given
T\gg 1
and small
\delta>0
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{2}-\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\delta}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathcal{E}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)dt\cdot\|\Psi\|_{L_{\omega}^{2}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\sigma_{0},p_{0}}},

(4.17)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{3}-\mathcal{B}_{3}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{-\frac{s}{2}+\delta}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathcal{E}(t)dt\cdot\|\Psi^{\prime}\Psi\|_{L_{\omega}^{2}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\sigma_{0},p_{0}}}

(4.18)

for any

0\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq T
,
uniformly in

N\in\mathbb{N}
and
M,T\gg 1
,
where

\sigma_{0}=\sigma_{0}(\delta)>0
and
p_{0}=p_{0}(\delta)\gg 1
are as in Lemma
2.3
.
Here,
\Psi^{\prime}
denotes an independent copy of
\Psi
.

Proof.

Proceeding as in (
4.16
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|\mathbb{E}[(I\Psi)(Iv)]Iv-I\big(\mathbb{E}[\Psi v]v\big)\big\|_{L^{2}_{\omega,x}}\\
&\quad\leq\Big\|\|(I\Psi(\omega^{\prime}))(Iv(\omega^{\prime}))(Iv(\omega))-I\big(\Psi(\omega^{\prime})v(\omega^{\prime})v(\omega)\big)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\Big\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}L^{1}_{\omega^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

(4.19)

Then,
the bound (
4.17
) follows
from
Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality (in
\omega
and
x
),
(
4.19
),
and Lemma
2.3

with (
4.11
).
The second bound (
4.18
)
follows in a similar manner.
∎

We conclude this section by estimating

\mathcal{B}_{3}^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\prime}
in (
4.16
).

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{3}^{\prime}
be as in (
4.16
).
Let
\frac{2}{3}\leq s<1
.
Then, given small
\gamma>0
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{3}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{\gamma}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathcal{E}^{\frac{3}{4}}(t)dt\cdot\|\Psi^{\prime}\Psi\|_{L_{\omega}^{2}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\gamma,\infty}}

(4.20)

for any

0\leq t_{0}\leq t\leq T
,
uniformly in

N\in\mathbb{N}
and
M,T\gg 1
.
Here,
\Psi^{\prime}
denotes an independent copy of
\Psi
.

Proof.

From the independence of
\Psi
and
\Psi^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|(\partial_{t}Iv(\omega))(Iv^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime}))\big(I(\Psi^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime})\Psi(\omega))\big)\big\|_{L^{1}_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}}\\
&\quad\leq\|\partial_{t}Iv\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}}\|Iv\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}}\big\|I(\Psi^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime})\Psi(\omega))\big)\big\|_{L^{2}_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}}\\
&\quad=\|\partial_{t}Iv\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}}\|Iv\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}}\big\|I(\Psi^{\prime}\Psi\big)\big\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}}.\end{split}

(4.21)

Then, the bound (
4.20
)
follows
from proceeding as in the proof of (
3.24
)
in Lemma
3.5

with (
4.21
).
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\prime}
be as in (
4.16
).
Then, given
s<1
,
there exists
c>0
such that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\prime}|\lesssim\bigg\{\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\Big(1+\mathcal{E}^{1+c\eta}(t)+\frac{\eta}{(t-t_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)dt\bigg\}\|I\Psi\|_{L^{\eta^{-1}}_{[t_{1},t_{2}],x}L^{2}_{\omega}}

(4.22)

for any

t_{2}\geq t_{1}\geq 0

and small
\eta>0
,
uniformly in

M,N\in\mathbb{N}
,
where

L^{r}_{[t_{1},t_{2}],x}=L^{r}_{[t_{1},t_{2}]}L^{r}_{x}
.

Proof.

The bound (
4.22
) follows
from a slight modification straightforward modification
of the proofs of

[
23
, Lemma 3.4]

and Lemma
3.6
,
and thus we only indicate required modifications.

By replacing the
\mathcal{A}_{N}
-norm in (
3.30
)
with the
L^{2}(\Omega)
-norm, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|Iv\|_{L^{\frac{4}{1-2\eta}}_{x}L^{2}_{\omega}}&\lesssim\|Iv\|_{L^{4}_{x}L^{2}_{\omega}}^{1-4\eta}\|Iv\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}H^{1}_{x}}^{4\eta}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{\frac{1+4\eta}{4}},\end{split}

(4.23)

uniformly in small
\eta>0
.
Moreover, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|(\partial_{t}Iv(\omega))\Big(2(I\Psi(\omega^{\prime}))(Iv(\omega^{\prime}))(Iv(\omega))+(Iv(\omega^{\prime}))^{2}](I\Psi(\omega))\Big)\Big\|_{L^{1}_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}L^{1}_{x}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\|\partial_{t}Iv\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{\omega}}\|Iv\|_{L^{\frac{4}{1-2\eta}}_{x}L^{2}_{\omega}}^{2}\|I\Psi\|_{L^{\eta^{-1}}_{x}L^{2}_{\omega}}.\end{split}

(4.24)

Then, the bound (
4.22
) follows from
repeating the rest of the argument in the proof of Lemma
3.6

with (
4.23
) and (
4.24
).
∎

5.
Mean-field limit of
the hyperbolic
O(N)
linear sigma model

In this section, we present a proof of Theorem
1.4

on convergence of
\text{HLSM}_{N}
(
1.10
)
to the mean-field SdNLW (
1.20
).
In Subsection
5.1
,
we present several law-of-large-numbers type lemmas.
In Subsection
5.2
,
we then prove Theorem
1.4
(i).
Lastly,
under a higher moment assumption,
we establish
a convergence rate
of (
1.10
)
to the mean-field limit
(Theorem
1.4
(ii))
in Subsection
5.3
.

5.1.
Law of large numbers

In this subsection, we present three
law-of-large-numbers type lemmas.
While the first lemma (Lemma
5.1
)
provides a convergence rate of the purely stochastic terms,
the second and third
lemmas (Lemmas
5.2
and
5.4
)
do not provide any rate
under the second moment assumption.
Under a higher moment assumption,
they can be upgraded
to versions with a decay rate;
see Lemmas
5.5
and
5.6
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Given
\ell=2,3
and
j,k\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
Z^{(\ell)}_{k,j}

be as in (
2.19
) or (
2.20
).
Namely,

\displaystyle Z_{k,j}^{(2)}=\,:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:\,\qquad\text{and}\qquad Z_{k,j}^{(3)}=\,:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}\Psi_{j}}\!:

(5.1)

or

\displaystyle Z_{k,j}^{(2)}=\,:\!{\Phi_{k}\Phi_{j}}\!:\,\qquad\text{and}\qquad Z_{k,j}^{(3)}=\,:\!{\Phi_{k}^{2}\Phi_{j}}\!:\,.

(5.2)

Then,
given
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}Z_{k,k}^{(2)}\,\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}L_{T}^{2}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}\frac{p}{N^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(5.3)

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}Z_{k,j}^{(3)}\,\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}L_{T}^{2}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}\frac{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}{N^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(5.4)

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}Z_{k,j}^{(3)}\,\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}\mathcal{A}_{N,j}L_{T}^{2}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}\frac{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}{N^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(5.5)

uniformly in finite
p\geq 1
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
where
\mathcal{A}_{N}B
is as in (
1.11
).
As a consequence, given any
\varepsilon_{0}>0
,
the following almost sure convergence holds
;

for each fixed
T>0
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}N^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}Z_{k,k}^{(2)}\,\bigg\|_{L_{T}^{2}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}&\longrightarrow 0,\\
\text{for each fixed $j\in\mathbb{N}:$}\quad N^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}Z_{k,j}^{(3)}\,\bigg\|_{L_{T}^{2}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}&\longrightarrow 0,\\
N^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}Z_{k,j}^{(3)}\,\bigg\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}L_{T}^{2}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}&\longrightarrow 0,\end{split}

(5.6)

as
N\to\infty
.

Proof.

By applying Chebyshev’s inequality
with (
5.3
), (
5.4
), and (
5.5
),
we see that
the almost sure convergence (
5.6
)
follows from the Borel-Cantelli lemma.
Hence,
we focus on proving (
5.3
), (
5.4
), and (
5.5
)
in the following.
Moreover, we assume
(
5.1
) since the same proof holds under (
5.2
).

We first look at (
5.3
).
By Sobolev’s embedding (with finite
r\gg 1
such that
\varepsilon r>4
),
Minkowski’s integral inequality
(for finite
p\geq r\geq 2
), the Wiener chaos estimate (Lemma
2.4
),
the independence
of
\{Z_{k,k}^{(2)}\}_{k=1}^{N}
,
and Lemma
2.5
(i), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}Z_{k,k}^{(2)}\,\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}L_{T}^{2}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}\lesssim\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\langle\nabla\rangle^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}Z_{k,k}^{(2)}\,\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}L_{T}^{2}L_{x}^{r}}\\
&\quad\leq\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\langle\nabla\rangle^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}Z_{k,k}^{(2)}(t,x)\bigg\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{x}^{r}L^{p}_{\omega}}\leq p\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\langle\nabla\rangle^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}Z_{k,k}^{(2)}(t,x)\bigg\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{x}^{r}L^{2}_{\omega}}\\
&\quad=\frac{p}{N^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}Z_{k,k}^{(2)}(t,x)\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{x}^{r}\mathcal{A}_{N}L^{2}_{\omega}}\lesssim_{T}\frac{p}{N^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}Z_{k,k}^{(2)}(t,x)\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}L^{2}_{\omega}C_{T}L_{x}^{r}}\\
&\quad\lesssim_{T}\frac{p}{N^{\frac{1}{2}}},\end{split}

(5.7)

yielding (
5.3
).

We note that (
5.5
) follows from (
5.4
)
and the definition (
1.11
) of the
\mathcal{A}_{N}B
-norm.
Thus, it suffices to prove (
5.4
).
Note from
(
2.12
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}\Psi_{j}}\!:\,:\!{\Psi_{k^{\prime}}^{2}\Psi_{j}}\!:\big]=0

unless
k=k^{\prime}
.
Then, the bound (
5.4
) follows
from proceeding
as in (
5.7
).
∎

In the following two lemmas,
we assume that

v_{k}\in L^{2}(\Omega;C([0,T];H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})))
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
which is the only integrability
guaranteed by Theorem
1.2

for global-in-time solutions to the mean-field SdNLW (
1.20
).
See Subsection
5.3

for a discussion under
a higher moment assumption.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\frac{1}{2}\leq s<1
.
Given
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
\Psi_{k}
be the stochastic convolution defined in (
2.10
)
and let
v_{k}\in L^{2}(\Omega;C([0,T];H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})))
be a solution of the mean-field SdNLW (
1.18
).
Moreover, we assume that
\big(v_{k}(0),\partial_{t}v_{k}(0)\big)\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
are independent and identically distributed over
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then, given
T>0

and small
\varepsilon>0
,
we have

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(\Psi_{k}v_{k}-\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{k}v_{k}]\Big)\bigg\|_{L_{T}^{2}W^{-\varepsilon,\frac{4}{2-s-\varepsilon}}_{x}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow 0,

(5.8)

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(v_{k}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[v_{k}^{2}]\Big)\bigg\|_{L_{T}^{2}(L^{\frac{3}{2-s}}_{x}\cap W_{x}^{\varepsilon,\frac{2}{2-s-\varepsilon}})}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow 0,

(5.9)

almost surely, as

N\to\infty
.

Proof.

We first note that

(\Psi_{k},v_{k})
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
, are independent
and identically distributed
since

\Psi_{k}
are independent
and identically distributed
and so are
the initial data

(v_{k}(0),\partial_{t}v_{k}(0))
.
In particular, we have

\displaystyle R\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{def}}}{{=}}\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\|v_{k}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}C_{T}H^{s}_{x}}=\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}C_{T}H^{s}_{x}}<\infty.

(5.10)

Proceeding as in (
4.8
) with
Lemma
2.1
(ii),
Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality, Lemma
2.5
(i),
Sobolev’s inequality,
and (
5.10
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\Psi_{k}v_{k}\|_{L_{T}^{2}W^{-\varepsilon,\frac{4}{2-s-\varepsilon}}_{x}}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Psi_{k}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}\|v_{k}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}C_{T}W_{x}^{\varepsilon,\frac{4}{2-s-\varepsilon}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v_{k}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}C_{T}H^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle<\infty,

uniformly in
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Hence, in view of the independence of
\{\Psi_{k}v_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
,
the almost sure convergence
(
5.8
) follows from the strong law of large numbers
in the Banach space setting
(
[
26
, Corollary 7.10 on p. 189]
).

Next, we consider (
5.9
).
Proceeding as in (
4.7
) and (
4.9
) with
the fractional Leibniz rule (Lemma
2.1
(i)),
Sobolev’s inequality, Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality,
and (
5.10
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\|v_{k}^{2}\|_{L_{T}^{2}(L^{\frac{3}{2-s}}_{x}\cap W_{x}^{\varepsilon,\frac{2}{2-s-\varepsilon}})}\Big]\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v_{k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\omega}C_{T}H_{x}^{s}}<\infty,

(5.11)

uniformly in
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Hence, in view of the independence of
\{v_{k}^{2}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
,
the almost sure convergence (
5.8
) follows from the strong law of large numbers (
[
26
, Corollary 7.10 on p. 189]
).
∎

Remark 5.3
.

From (
5.11
), we see that
the
L^{2}(\Omega)
-integrability assumption
for
v_{k}

is indeed necessary
for the (almost sure) convergence (
5.9
);
see
[
26
, Corollary 7.10 on p. 189]
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\frac{1}{2}\leq s<1

and
\{(\Psi_{j},v_{j})\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
be as in Lemma
5.2
.
Then, given
T>0
,
we have, for each
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(v_{k}\!:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:-\mathbb{E}[v_{k}\Psi_{k}]\Psi_{j}\Big)\bigg\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{x}^{s-1}}\longrightarrow 0

(5.12)

in probability as
N\to\infty
,
and

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(v_{k}\!:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:-\mathbb{E}[v_{k}\Psi_{k}]\Psi_{j}\Big)\bigg\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}L_{T}^{2}H_{x}^{s-1}}\longrightarrow 0

(5.13)

in probability as
N\to\infty
.

Proof.

We only prove (
5.13
) in the following, since (
5.12
)
follows in a similar manner.

We first study the contribution to (
5.13
) from the case
k=j
.
Let
1<p<2
.
Then,
proceeding as in (
3.10
)
with Hölder’s inequality,
Lemma
2.5
(i), and (
5.10
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Big\|\|v_{j}\!:\!{\Psi_{j}^{2}}\!:\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{x}^{s-1}}\Big\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}}&\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}C_{T}H_{x}^{s}}\|:\!{\Psi_{j}^{2}}\!:\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{2-p}}_{\omega}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}\\
&\lesssim_{p}T^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

(5.14)

uniformly in
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
provided that
0<\varepsilon\leq\min(1-s,s)
.
Let
(\Psi^{\prime},v^{\prime})
be an independent copy of
(\Psi_{j},v_{j})
.
Then, given
1<p<2
,
it follows from
Minkowski’s integral inequality,
the independence of
(\Psi^{\prime},v^{\prime})
from
\Psi_{j}
,
and proceeding as in (
5.14
)
with (
4.5
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Big\|\|\mathbb{E}[v_{j}\Psi_{j}]\Psi_{j}\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{x}^{s-1}}\Big\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}}&=\Big\|\|\mathbb{E}_{\omega^{\prime}}[v^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime})\Psi^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime})]\Psi_{j}(\omega)\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{x}^{s-1}}\Big\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}}\\
&\leq\|v^{\prime}\Psi^{\prime}\Psi_{j}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}L_{T}^{2}H_{x}^{s-1}}\\
&\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}C_{T}H_{x}^{s}}\|\Psi^{\prime}\Psi_{j}\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{2-p}}_{\omega}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}\\
&\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

(5.15)

uniformly in
j\in\mathbb{N}
.
Hence,
given
L\gg 1
, it follows
from Chebyshev’s inequality with (
5.14
) and (
5.15
)
that

\displaystyle\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\Big(v_{j}:\!{\Psi_{j}^{2}}\!:-\mathbb{E}[v_{j}\Psi_{j}]\Psi_{j}\Big)\bigg\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}L_{T}^{2}H_{x}^{s-1}}>\frac{1}{L}\Bigg)\lesssim_{T}\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\frac{L^{p}}{N^{p}}<\infty,

(5.16)

since
p>1
.
Therefore, by the Borel-Cantelli lemma, we conclude that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\Big(v_{j}:\!{\Psi_{j}^{2}}\!:-\mathbb{E}[v_{j}\Psi_{j}]\Psi_{j}\Big)\bigg\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}L_{T}^{2}H_{x}^{s-1}}\longrightarrow 0,

almost surely (and thus in probability) as
N\to\infty
,
which shows (
5.13
)
for the case
k=j
.

Next, we study the contribution to (
5.13
) from the case
k\neq j
.
By taking the Fourier transform in
x
(suppressing the
t
-dependence), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{x}\Big(\big(v_{k}\Psi_{k}-\mathbb{E}[v_{k}\Psi_{k}]\big)\Psi_{j}\Big)(n)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{n=n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}}\Big(\widehat{v}_{k}(n_{1})\widehat{\Psi}_{k}(n_{2})-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{v}_{k}(n_{1})\widehat{\Psi}_{k}(n_{2})]\Big)\widehat{\Psi}_{j}(n_{3}).

Then, given
M\gg 1
(to be chosen later), we write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1\\
k\neq j\end{subarray}}^{N}\big(v_{k}\Psi_{k}-\mathbb{E}[v_{k}\Psi_{k}]\big)\Psi_{j}=\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{j}+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{j}

(5.17)

where

\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{j}
denotes the contribution
from the case

\displaystyle\max(|n|,|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|,|n_{3}|)>M.

(5.18)

Namely,
\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{j}
denotes the contribution from the case:

\max(|n|,|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|,|n_{3}|)\leq M
, given by

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{j}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1\\
k\neq j\end{subarray}}^{N}\mathbf{P}_{M}\Big(\big(\mathbf{P}_{M}v_{k}\Psi_{k,M}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{P}_{M}v_{k}\Psi_{k,M}]\big)\Psi_{j,M}\Big),

(5.19)

where

\Psi_{j,M}=\mathbf{P}_{M}\Psi_{j}
.

We first treat the first term
\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{j}
.
From a slight modification of (
3.10
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|v_{k}\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{s-1+\varepsilon}_{x}}&\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v_{k}\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}\|_{C_{T}W_{x}^{-2\varepsilon,\frac{2}{2-s-3\varepsilon}}}\\
&\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v_{k}\|_{C_{T}W_{x}^{2\varepsilon,\frac{2}{2-s-3\varepsilon}}}\|\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}\|_{C_{T}W^{-2\varepsilon,\infty}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v_{k}\|_{C_{T}H_{x}^{s-\varepsilon}}\|\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}\|_{C_{T}W^{-2\varepsilon,\infty}_{x}}.\end{split}

(5.20)

Note that, thanks to (
5.18
), we can gain
M^{-\varepsilon}

by losing
\varepsilon
-regularity.
Thus, from
(
5.20
), (
5.18
),
Lemma
2.5
(ii)
(see also (
2.24
) in Remark
2.6
(ii)),
(
5.20
),
Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality,
and (
5.10
),
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{j}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}L^{2}_{T}H^{s-1}_{x}}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}M^{-\varepsilon}R,

uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then, given small
\delta>0
,
it follows from Markov’s inequality
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\|\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{j}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}L^{2}_{T}H^{s-1}_{x}}>\delta\Big)\leq C_{T}\frac{R}{\delta M^{\varepsilon}}<\frac{\delta}{3},

(5.21)

uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
by taking

M=M(\delta,T,R)\gg 1

sufficiently large.

Next, we consider the term
\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{j}
in (
5.19
),
where all the frequencies are bounded by
M
.
We first write
\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{j}
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{j}&=\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}^{(1)}_{j}+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}^{(2)}_{j}\\
&\hskip-0.85358pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{def}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathbf{P}_{M}\Big(\big(\mathbf{P}_{M}v_{k}\Psi_{k,M}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{P}_{M}v_{k}\Psi_{k,M}]\big)\Psi_{j,M}\Big)\\
&\quad\,-\frac{1}{N}\mathbf{P}_{M}\Big(\big(\mathbf{P}_{M}v_{j}\Psi_{j,M}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{P}_{M}v_{j}\Psi_{j,M}]\big)\Psi_{j,M}\Big).\end{split}

(5.22)

Let us first consider
\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}^{(2)}_{j}
.
By a slight modification of (
5.14
),
for
1<p<2
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Big\|\|\mathbf{P}_{M}v_{j}\Psi_{j,M}^{2}\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{x}^{s-1}}\Big\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}}&\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}M^{2\varepsilon}\|v_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}C_{T}H_{x}^{s}}\|\Psi_{j}\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{4p}{2-p}}_{\omega}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}\\
&\lesssim_{p}T^{\frac{1}{2}}M^{2\varepsilon},\end{split}

(5.23)

uniformly in
j\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then, from (
5.23
) and (
5.15
)
(which implies
(
5.16
) with an extra factor
M^{2\varepsilon p}
on the right-hand side)
with the Borel-Cantelli lemma, we conclude that

\displaystyle\big\|\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}^{(2)}_{j}\big\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}L_{T}^{2}H_{x}^{s-1}}\longrightarrow 0,

almost surely (and thus in probability) as
N\to\infty
.
Namely, given small
\delta>0
,
there exists
N_{1}=N_{1}(\delta)\gg 1
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\big\|\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}^{(2)}_{j}\big\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}L_{T}^{2}H_{x}^{s-1}}>\delta\Big)<\frac{\delta}{3}

(5.24)

for any
N\geq N_{1}
.

Next, we treat
\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}^{(1)}_{j}
in (
5.22
).
From (
5.10
)
and Lemma
2.5
(i), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\mathbf{P}_{M}v_{k}\Psi_{k,M}\|_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{\varepsilon}T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}C_{T}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Psi_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}C_{T}W^{-\varepsilon,\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle<\infty,

uniformly in
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then,
noting
that
\mathbf{P}_{M}v_{k}\Psi_{k,M}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{P}_{M}v_{k}\Psi_{k,M}]
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
are independent and identically distributed,
it follows from
[
26
, Corollary 7.10 on p. 189]

that

\displaystyle Y_{N,M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{def}}}{{=}}\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\big(\mathbf{P}_{M}v_{k}\Psi_{k,M}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{P}_{M}v_{k}\Psi_{k,M}]\big)\bigg\|_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}\longrightarrow 0,

(5.25)

almost surely, as
N\to\infty
,
which in particular implies that the convergence (
5.25
)
also holds in probability.
Thus, given small
\delta>0
and
K\gg 1
, there exists
N_{2}=N_{2}(\delta,M,K)\gg 1
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(Y_{N,M}>\delta K^{-1}\Big)<\frac{\delta}{6}

(5.26)

for any
N\geq N_{2}
.
By a crude estimate, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{j}^{(1)}\big\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}L^{2}_{T}H^{s-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}M^{\varepsilon}Y_{N,M}\|\Psi_{j}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}C_{T}W^{-\varepsilon,\infty}_{x}}.

(5.27)

Given small
\delta>0
,
T>0
, and
M\gg 1
,
it follows from
Lemma
2.5
(ii)
that
there exists
K=K(\delta,M,T)\gg 1
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(C_{0}M^{\varepsilon}\|\Psi_{j}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}C_{T}W^{-\varepsilon,\infty}_{x}}>K\Big)<\frac{\delta}{6},

(5.28)

uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
Hence, given small
\delta>0
,
it follows from (
5.27
)
with (
5.26
) and (
5.28
)
that
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{P}&\Big(\big\|\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{j}^{(1)}\big\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}L^{2}_{T}H^{s-1}_{x}}>\delta\Big)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(Y_{N,M}>\delta K^{-1}\Big)\\
&\quad+\mathbb{P}\Big(\{Y_{N,M}\leq\delta K^{-1}\}\cap\{C_{0}M^{\varepsilon}\|\Psi_{j}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}C_{T}W^{-\varepsilon,\infty}_{x}}>K\}\Big)\\
&<\frac{\delta}{6}+\mathbb{P}\Big(C_{0}M^{\varepsilon}\|\Psi_{j}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}C_{T}W^{-\varepsilon,\infty}_{x}}>K\Big)\\
&<\frac{\delta}{3}\end{split}

(5.29)

for any
N\geq N_{2}
.

Therefore, from (
5.17
), (
5.21
), (
5.22
), (
5.24
), and (
5.29
),
we conclude
(
5.13
)
for the case
k\neq j
.
∎

5.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.4
(i)

In this subsection,
we present a proof of Theorem
1.4
(i).

Let
\frac{4}{5}<s<1
.
Let

\big\{\big(u_{N,j}^{(0)},u_{N,j}^{(1)}\big)\big\}_{j=1}^{N}\in\big(\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\big)^{\otimes N}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
and

\big\{\big(u_{j}^{(0)},u_{j}^{(1)}\big)\big\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\big(\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\big)^{\otimes\mathbb{N}}

be sequences of random functions
as in
Theorem
1.4
(i),
where the former converges in probability to the latter in the sense
of (
1.24
) and (
1.25
).
In particular, we assume that

\big(u_{j}^{(0)},u_{j}^{(1)}\big)\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
,
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
are independent and identically distributed.

Let

\{(u_{N,j},\partial_{t}u_{N,j})\}_{j=1}^{N}

and
\{(u_{j},\partial_{t}u_{j})\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}

be the global-in-time solutions to (
1.10
)
and (
1.20
)
with initial data
\big\{\big(u_{N,j}^{(0)},u_{N,j}^{(1)}\big)\big\}_{j=1}^{N}

and
\big\{\big(u_{j}^{(0)},u_{j}^{(1)}\big)\big\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}

constructed in Theorems
1.1

and
1.2
, respectively.
Then, set

v_{N,j}=u_{N,j}-\Psi_{j}

and

v_{j}=u_{j}-\Psi_{j}

as in (
1.7
)
and (
1.19
)
such that

\{(v_{N,j},\partial_{t}v_{N,j})\}_{j=1}^{N}\in\big(C(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))\big)^{\otimes N}

almost surely
and

(v_{j},\partial_{t}v_{j})\in L^{2}(\Omega;C([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})))

for any
T>0
.
Then,
by writing (
1.8
) and (
1.18
) in the Duhamel formulation, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}v_{N,j}&=S(t)\big(u_{N,j}^{(0)},u_{N,j}^{(1)}\big)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathcal{I}\Big(v_{N,k}^{2}v_{N,j}+2\Psi_{k}v_{N,k}v_{N,j}+v_{N,k}^{2}\Psi_{j}\\
&\hphantom{XXXXXXXXXXX}+:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}}\!:v_{N,j}+2v_{N,k}:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:+:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}\Psi_{j}}\!:\Big)\end{split}

(5.30)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}v_{j}&=S(t)\big(u_{j}^{(0)},u_{j}^{(1)}\big)\\
&\quad-\mathcal{I}\Big(\mathbb{E}[v_{j}^{2}]v_{j}+2\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{j}v_{j}]v_{j}+\mathbb{E}[v_{j}^{2}]\Psi_{j}+2\mathbb{E}[v_{j}\Psi_{j}]\Psi_{j}\Big),\end{split}

(5.31)

where
S(t)
and
\mathcal{I}
are as in (
2.5
) and (
2.6
),
respectively.
We set

\displaystyle V_{N,j}=v_{N,j}-v_{j}

(5.32)

and
\vec{V}_{N,j}=(V_{N,j},\partial_{t}V_{N,j})
.
By taking the difference of (
5.30
) and (
5.31
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}V_{N,j}(t)&=S(t)(\vec{V}_{N,j}(0))-\sum_{\ell=1}^{6}\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{A}_{j,\ell})(t)\\
&=S(t)\big(u_{N,j}^{(0)}-u_{j}^{(0)},u_{N,j}^{(1)}-u_{j}^{(1)}\big)-\sum_{\ell=1}^{6}\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{A}_{j,\ell})(t),\end{split}

(5.33)

where

\{\mathcal{A}_{j,\ell}=A_{j,\ell}(N)\}_{j=1}^{N}
,
\ell=1,\dots,6
, are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{A}_{j,1}&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}v_{N,k}^{2}v_{N,j}-\mathbb{E}[v_{j}^{2}]v_{j},\\
\mathcal{A}_{j,2}&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}2\Psi_{k}v_{N,k}v_{N,j}-2\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{j}v_{j}]v_{j},\\
\mathcal{A}_{j,3}&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}v_{N,k}^{2}\Psi_{j}-\mathbb{E}[v_{j}^{2}]\Psi_{j},\\
\mathcal{A}_{j,4}&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}}\!:v_{N,j},\\
\mathcal{A}_{j,5}&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}2v_{N,k}:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:-2\mathbb{E}[v_{j}\Psi_{j}]\Psi_{j},\\
\mathcal{A}_{j,6}&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}\Psi_{j}}\!:.\end{split}

(5.34)

Fix a target time
T\gg 1

and we work on the interval
[0,T]
.
We point out the following;
while
we take
\frac{4}{5}<s<1

to prove global-in-time convergence
(namely, on
[0,T]
for any given
T\gg 1
),
the convergence argument presented below works for

\frac{1}{2}\leq s<1
,

assuming
that

v_{j}
exists on
[0,T]

(more precisely,
(v_{j},\partial_{t}v_{j})\in L^{2}(\Omega;C([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2})))
).

Since
the parameters
s,\varepsilon,T
are fixed, we often drop the dependence
on these parameters.
In the remaining part of this subsection,
we fix small
\delta>0
.

\bullet

Step 1:

In this first step, we establish the convergence (
1.27
)
for each
T\gg 1
:

\displaystyle\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(T)}=\|\vec{v}_{N,j}-\vec{v}_{j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(T)}\longrightarrow 0

(5.35)

in probability as
N\to\infty
,
where
X^{s}_{N}(T)
is as in (
3.3
).

Before we start estimating the terms on the right-hand side of (
5.33
),
let us first prepare
data sets indexed by
N\gg 1
.
As we noted in the proof of Lemma
5.2
,
the solutions
\vec{v}_{j}=(v_{j},\partial_{t}v)\in C([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))

to (
1.18
),
j\in\mathbb{N}
, are
independent
and identically distributed.
In particular, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\|\vec{v}_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}=\|\vec{v}_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}<\infty.

(5.36)

Given
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
R\geq 1
, define
the set
\Omega_{1,N}(R)\subset\Omega

by setting

\displaystyle\Omega_{1,N}(R)=\Big\{\omega\in\Omega:\|\vec{v}_{j}\|_{X_{N}^{s}(T)}\leq R\Big\}.

(5.37)

Then, it follows from Chebyshev’s inequality,
(
3.3
),
and (
5.36
) that
there exists
R=R(\delta)\gg 1
, independent of
N\in\mathbb{N}
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big((\Omega_{1,N}(R))^{c}\big)<\frac{\delta}{8}.

(5.38)

Given small
\eta_{0}>0
,
define
\Omega_{2,N}(\eta_{0})\subset\Omega
by setting

\displaystyle\Omega_{2,N}(\eta_{0})=\big\{\omega\in\Omega:\|\vec{V}_{N,j}(0)\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}=\big\|\big(u_{N,j}^{(0)}-u_{j}^{(0)},u_{N,j}^{(1)}-u_{j}^{(1)}\big)\big\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}\leq\eta_{0}\Big\}.

(5.39)

Then, from
the assumption (
1.25
) on the initial data,
there exists
N_{1}=N_{1}(\eta_{0},\delta)\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big((\Omega_{2,N}(\eta_{0}))^{c}\big)<\frac{\delta}{8}.

(5.40)

for any
N\geq N_{1}
.

Next, we discuss controls on the stochastic terms.
Given
K\geq 1
, define the set
\Omega_{3,N}(K)\subset\Omega
by setting

\displaystyle\Omega_{3,N}(K)=\Big\{\omega\in\Omega:\|\Psi_{j}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}C_{T}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}+\|:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:\|_{\mathcal{A}^{(2)}_{N}C_{T}W^{-\varepsilon,\infty}_{x}}\leq K\Big\}.

(5.41)

Then, it follows from Lemma
2.5
(ii)
and Chebyshev’s inequality
that
there exists
K=K(\delta)\gg 1
, independent of
N\in\mathbb{N}
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big((\Omega_{3,N}(K))^{c}\big)<\frac{\delta}{8}.

(5.42)

Given small
\eta>0
,
define the set

\Omega_{4,N}(\eta)\subset\Omega
by setting

\displaystyle\Omega_{4,N}(\eta)=\Big\{\omega\in\Omega:\|(\mathbf{v},\boldsymbol{\Psi})\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{N}}\leq\eta\Big\},

(5.43)

where the
\mathcal{Y}_{N}
-norm is defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|(\mathbf{v},\boldsymbol{\Psi})\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{N}}&=\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}}\!:\bigg\|_{L_{T}^{2}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}+\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}\Psi_{j}}\!:\bigg\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}L_{T}^{2}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}\\
&\quad+\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(\Psi_{k}v_{k}-\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{k}v_{k}]\Big)\bigg\|_{L_{T}^{2}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\frac{4}{2-s-\varepsilon}}}\\
&\quad+\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(v_{k}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[v_{k}^{2}]\Big)\bigg\|_{L_{T}^{2}(L^{\frac{3}{2-s}}_{x}\cap W_{x}^{\varepsilon,\frac{2}{2-s-\varepsilon}})}\\
&\quad+\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(v_{k}\!:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:-\mathbb{E}[v_{k}\Psi_{k}]\Psi_{j}\Big)\bigg\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}L_{T}^{2}H_{x}^{s-1}}.\end{split}

(5.44)

Then,
it follows from Lemmas
5.1
,
5.2
, and
5.4

that there exists
N_{2}=N_{2}(\eta,\delta)\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big((\Omega_{4,N}(\eta))^{c}\big)<\frac{\delta}{8}

(5.45)

for
N\geq N_{2}
.

We are now ready to estimate the terms on the right-hand side of (
5.33
).
Let
J\subset[0,T]
be an interval
with
|J|\leq 1
.
With a slight abuse of notation,
we define

X^{s}_{N}(J)
by setting

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\vec{v}_{j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}&=\|(v_{j},\partial_{t}v_{j})\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}C_{J}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}\\
&=\|(v_{j},\partial_{t}v_{j})\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}C_{J}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}},\end{split}

(5.46)

where the
\mathcal{A}_{N}B
-norm is as in (
1.11
).
In the following, we work on

\Omega_{N}(R,K,\eta_{0},\eta)
defined by

\displaystyle\Omega_{N}(R,K,\eta_{0},\eta)=\Omega_{1,N}(R)\cap\Omega_{2,N}(\eta_{0})\cap\Omega_{3,N}(K)\cap\Omega_{4,N}(\eta),

(5.47)

where

\Omega_{1,N}(R)
,
\Omega_{2,N}(\eta_{0})
,
\Omega_{3,N}(K)
, and
\Omega_{4,N}(\eta)

are as in (
5.37
), (
5.39
), (
5.41
), and (
5.43
),
respectively.
Before proceeding further, we note that, given small
\delta>0
,
by choosing

R=R(\delta)

and
K=K(\delta)
sufficiently large,
it follows from
(
5.47
)
with (
5.38
), (
5.40
), (
5.42
), and (
5.45
)
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big((\Omega_{N}(R,K,\eta_{0},\eta))^{c}\big)<\frac{\delta}{2}

(5.48)

for any
N\geq\max(N_{1},N_{2})
,
where
N_{1}(\eta_{0},\delta)

and
N_{2}(\eta,\delta)

are as in (
5.40
)
and (
5.45
), respectively.

From (
5.34
) with (
5.32
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{A}_{j,1}&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}V_{N,k}v_{N,k}v_{N,j}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}v_{k}V_{N,k}v_{N,j}\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}v_{k}v_{k}V_{N,j}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(v_{k}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[v_{k}^{2}]\Big)v_{j}.\end{split}

(5.49)

Using (
5.32
),
we replace each occurrence of
v_{N,\ell}
in (
5.49
) by
V_{N,\ell}+v_{\ell}
,
\ell=j,k
.
Then,
by proceeding as in (
3.6
)
(while keeping the
k
-summation inside for the last term
in (
5.49
))
with (
5.37
) and (
5.43
),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\vec{\mathcal{I}}(\mathcal{A}_{j,1})\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}&\lesssim|J|\Big(\|\vec{V}_{N,k}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}^{2}+\|\vec{v}_{k}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}^{2}\Big)\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}\\
&\quad+|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(v_{k}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[v_{k}^{2}]\Big)\bigg\|_{L_{J}^{2}L^{\frac{3}{2-s}}_{x}}\|\vec{v}_{j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}\\
&\leq|J|\Big(\|\vec{V}_{N,k}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}^{2}+R^{2}\Big)\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}+|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta R,\end{split}

(5.50)

provided that
\frac{1}{2}\leq s
(
<1
).

From (
5.34
) with (
5.32
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{A}_{j,2}&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}2\Psi_{k}V_{N,k}v_{N,j}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}2\Psi_{k}v_{k}V_{N,j}\\
&\quad+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(\Psi_{k}v_{k}-\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{k}v_{k}]\Big)v_{j}.\end{split}

(5.51)

Then,
by proceeding as in (
3.8
) (see also (
4.8
))
with Lemma
2.1
(ii),
Sobolev’s inequality,
(
5.37
),
(
5.41
),
and (
5.43
),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|&\vec{\mathcal{I}}(\mathcal{A}_{j,2})\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}\\
&\lesssim|J|\|\Psi_{k}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}C_{J}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}\Big(\|\vec{V}_{N,k}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}+\|\vec{v}_{k}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}\Big)\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}\\
&\quad+|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(\Psi_{k}v_{k}-\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{k}v_{k}]\Big)\bigg\|_{L^{2}_{J}W^{-\varepsilon,\frac{4}{2-s-\varepsilon}}_{x}}\|v_{j}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}C_{J}W^{\varepsilon,\frac{4}{2-s-\varepsilon}}_{x}}\\
&\leq|J|K\Big(\|\vec{V}_{N,k}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}+R\Big)\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}+|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta R,\end{split}

(5.52)

provided that
3\varepsilon\leq s
(
<1
).
Similarly, by
writing

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{j,3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}V_{N,k}v_{N,k}\Psi_{j}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}v_{k}V_{N,k}\Psi_{j}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(v_{k}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[v_{k}^{2}]\Big)\Psi_{j}

(5.53)

and proceeding as in (
5.52
) (see also (
4.9
)) with
(
5.37
),
(
5.41
),
and
(
5.43
),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|&\vec{\mathcal{I}}(\mathcal{A}_{j,3})\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}\\
&\lesssim|J|\Big(\|\vec{V}_{N,k}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}+\|\vec{v}_{k}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}\Big)\|\vec{V}_{N,k}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}\|\Psi_{j}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}C_{J}W_{x}^{-\varepsilon,\infty}}\\
&\quad+|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(v_{k}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[v_{k}^{2}]\Big)\bigg\|_{L_{J}^{2}W_{x}^{\varepsilon,\frac{2}{2-s-\varepsilon}}}\|\Psi_{j}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}C_{J}W^{-\varepsilon,\infty}_{x}}\\
&\leq|J|K\Big(\|\vec{V}_{N,k}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}+R\Big)\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}+|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta K.\end{split}

(5.54)

Proceeding as in (
3.10
)
with
(
5.37
)
and
(
5.43
),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\vec{\mathcal{I}}(\mathcal{A}_{j,4})\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}&\lesssim|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}}\!:\bigg\|_{L^{2}_{T}W^{-\varepsilon,\infty}_{x}}\Big(\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}+\|\vec{v}_{j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}\Big)\\
&\lesssim|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}+|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta R,\end{split}

(5.55)

provided that
\varepsilon\leq s\leq 1-\varepsilon
.
By writing

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{j,5}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}V_{N,k}:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(v_{k}:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:-\mathbb{E}[v_{k}\Psi_{k}]\Psi_{j}\Big)

(5.56)

and
proceeding as in (
3.10
)
with
(
5.41
)
and
(
5.43
),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\vec{\mathcal{I}}(\mathcal{A}_{j,5})\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}&\lesssim|J|\|:\!{\Psi_{k}\Psi_{j}}\!:\|_{\mathcal{A}^{(2)}_{N}C_{J}W^{-\varepsilon,\infty}_{x}}\|\vec{V}_{N,k}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}+|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta\\
&\leq|J|K\|\vec{V}_{N,k}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}+|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta,\end{split}

(5.57)

where
A^{(2)}_{N}B
is as in (
2.2
).
Lastly, from (
5.34
)
and
(
5.43
),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\vec{\mathcal{I}}(\mathcal{A}_{j,6})\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}&\lesssim|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}:\!{\Psi_{k}^{2}\Psi_{j}}\!:\bigg\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N,j}L^{2}_{J}H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
&\leq|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta.\end{split}

(5.58)

Therefore,
putting (
5.33
)
(but starting at time
t_{0}
),
(
5.50
),
(
5.52
), (
5.54
), (
5.55
),
(
5.57
), and (
5.58
)
together
with
J=[t_{0},t_{1}]\subset[0,T]

such that
|J|\leq 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}&\leq C_{1}\|\vec{V}_{N,j}(t_{0})\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}\\
&\quad+C_{2}|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}K\Big(\|\vec{V}_{N,k}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}^{2}+R^{2}\Big)\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}\\
&\quad+C_{3}|J|^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta(R+K).\end{split}

(5.59)

Suppose that

\displaystyle\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}\leq 1

(5.60)

for some interval
J\subset[0,T]
.
Then,
it follows from (
5.59
) that
there exists
\tau=\tau(R,K)\in(0,1]
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J)}&\leq 2C_{1}\|\vec{V}_{N,j}(t_{0})\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}+2C_{3}\eta,\end{split}

(5.61)

provided that
|J|\leq\tau
.

While the claimed convergence-in-probability
(
5.35
)
follows from
(
5.48
),
the discussion above,
(
5.39
) (with sufficiently small
\eta_{0}>0
),
and a standard continuity argument,
we present some details for readers’ convenience,
especially similar iterative arguments
are needed in Step 2 and in Subsection
6.2
.

Fix small
\delta>0
.
With
R=R(\delta)\gg 1
and
K=K(\delta)\gg 1

as in (
5.38
) and (
5.42
),
let
\tau=\tau(R(\delta),K(\delta))=\tau(\delta)>0

as in (
5.61
).
Then, write
[0,T]
as

\displaystyle[0,T]=\bigcup_{\ell=0}^{[T/\tau]}J_{\ell},

where
J_{\ell}=[\ell\tau,(\ell+1)\tau]\cap[0,T]
.
Let us work on the first interval
J_{0}=[0,\tau]
.
Note from (
5.39
) that

\displaystyle\|\vec{V}_{N,j}(0)\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}\leq\eta_{0}\ll 1.

(5.62)

Then, in view of the continuity
of
\|V_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}([0,t])}

in
t
,
it follows from a standard continuity argument
with (
5.60
), (
5.61
), and (
5.62
)
that

\displaystyle\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J_{0})}\leq 2C_{1}\eta_{0}+2C_{3}\eta,

which in turn implies

\displaystyle\|\vec{V}_{N,j}(\tau)\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}\leq 2C_{1}\eta_{0}+2C_{3}\eta\ll 1.

(5.63)

As a consequence,
it follows from (
5.63
), (
5.61
), and a continuity argument
that

\displaystyle\|\vec{V}_{N,j}(2\tau)\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}\leq\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J_{1})}\leq(2C_{1})^{2}\eta_{0}+2C_{3}(2C_{1}+1)\eta\ll 1,

which allows us to iterate the argument on the third interval
J_{2}
.

After
\ell+1
steps, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\vec{V}_{N,j}((\ell+1)\tau)\|_{\mathcal{A}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{x}}\leq\|\vec{V}_{N,j}\|_{X^{s}_{N}(J_{\ell})}&\leq(2C_{1})^{\ell}\eta_{0}+2C_{3}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{\ell-1}(2C_{1})^{\ell^{\prime}}\cdot\eta\\
&\leq(2C_{1})^{\ell}\eta_{0}+2C_{3}\frac{(2C_{1})^{\ell}}{2C_{1}-1}\eta.\end{split}

(5.64)

In order to cover the entire interval
[0,T]
,
recalling that
\tau
depends only on
\delta
,
it suffices to choose

\eta_{0}=\eta_{0}(\delta)
and
\eta=\eta(\delta)
sufficiently small
such that
